(a) Oracle verified by §5.1 part 1 over all rows and all target values for N ≤ 2¹⁰, fault rate zero. (b) End-to-end Grover by state-vector simulation with branch enumeration at N ≤ 2⁸. (c) A resource estimate for N = 2²⁰ under a named target, reporting the budget and the padded cost. Nobody rewrote SQLite. |
| 5 | Julia on a shim runtime | `push!` and `Dict` work because the allocator works, with zero compiler awareness of either; forward results equal to native Julia; reversal exact. |
| 6 | xv6, then Linux `tinyconfig` | Serial console output equal to a native (QEMU) run of the same image (independent forward check); boots in the reversible tier with device models; un-boots to the reset vector; tape replay byte-identical. |
| 7 | **Quantum fuzzing of the kernel** | Kernel state snapshot fixed classically after boot; a step budget; syscall arguments as the superposed input over a declared small space; devices virtualised; the oracle bit is the panic flag. The contract is the optimised-IR reference semantics of §3.2 under a recorded definedness assumption; the kernel's own source UB is not detected after optimisation and is not claimed to be. (a) Oracle verified by §5.1 part 1 against native execution of the same snapshot per basis input, with classifier-flagged inputs reported, fault rate zero on the declared space. (b) Amplitude amplification by state-vector simulation at a toy argument space. (c) A resource estimate under a named target. The oracle is Linux. |
| 8 | **Amplitude estimation on a Monte Carlo in plain C** | Written by someone who has never heard of a Toffoli gate. The entropy device is replaced by a superposed register; everything else is fixed classically before entry. (a) Oracle by §5.1 part 1; (b) estimation by simulation at small precision; (c) resource estimate. |

Rungs 1–3 and 5–6 are the blueprint's; 4, 7, 8 define success for the
vision; nobody promises 6–8 on a schedule.

## 9. Open technical questions (deferred by the maintainer's rule: vision first)

In the order the next iteration should take them. Items the council
promoted from "detail" to "load-bearing" are now answered at vision level
above and appear here only for their mechanics.

1. **The legality spike** (§3.2): the marker corpus against every stock
   pass at O3 and through native codegen; emission examples from clang,
   rustc and Julia; gate on freezing the dialect spec.
2. **Handle → wire conversion**: the verifier, what it rejects (including
   the uncomputation-validity invariant and the copy/store/in-place rules
   of §4.2), the leak check.
3. **Compilation model mechanics** (§3.3): LTO extension point vs `opt`;
   fat-object layout; the launch ABI; host-triple intrinsic and asm
   classification tables; bitcode builds of language runtimes.
4. **Budget mechanics**: out-of-band budget tables; scalar-evolution
   refinement; how the specialiser recovers structure from the step
   machine; the exact fault-code set.
5. **Effect virtualisation**: which device models exist (VFS, console,
   clock, entropy, block device, network as a recorded peer), how they are
   expressed as reversible state, what is refused.
6. **Measurement-based uncomputation under control**: the v1
   `PermClean`-style certificate (inherited from the sunset Sturm.jl)
   cannot express it; the certificate kind with classical fixup and its
   controlled form, fixed in Bennett.jl's language before any gate record
   freezes.
7. **Reversible ABI and linking**: call/uncall convention, shared ancilla
   frames, structural symbol resolution, content-addressed objects.
8. **Run-time scheduling policy**: history layers, checkpoint interval,
   delta quality, recomputation of coherent regions, as resource policy
   under the vector of §4.3.
9. **ISA serialisation and versioning**; conformance suite; the formal
   model's scope.
10. **Per-LLVM-major builds**; Julia in-process invocation through LLVM.jl.
11. Concurrency (sequentialised with a recorded schedule; the single-
    thread assumption of §3.2 is the v1 contract), self-modifying code,
    signals, setjmp. Floating point is decided in §3.2 (deterministic
    soft-float in both builds) and is no longer open.
12. Repository hosting and release mechanics for §6.1.

## 10. Process

Vision first (this document), iterate with the council, then design, then
spikes, then code. The current Bennett.jl and BennettVM.jl are frozen as
the v1 reference and differential oracle. No reboot code is written on the
strength of this document. Worklog, beads, red-green, exhaustive
verification, no remote CI carry over unchanged.


# Background (not graded): `fat-object-scope.md`

# Mixed fat object — shared contract scope

Proposal for every revision in this directory; not a published ISA amendment.
Clause references are to [ISA 0.9](../../../spec/Bennett-ISA-Spec-0.9.md),
[the North Star](../../../../bennett/docs/Bennett-NorthStar.md), and, where
qualified, the maintained dialect 0.2/0.3 texts. This is the shared statement
required by bennett-4ht and the council rubric's standing mixed-fat-object check.

## Citable scoping statement

> A mixed fat object retains an ordinary native host binary and embeds ISA
> objects for marked device modules and their linked device closures (North
> Star §§2, 3.3, 4.4). ISA §§9.1–9.3 identify each device object and selected
> root; §12.3 certificates, owner records and reports cover that closure,
> with a separate binding/context set per device root and [] relative to
> that root (§12.1). Native entry, argv/envp and host-only libc need no ISA
> region, frame or certificate record; native comparison binds the selected
> materialised closure, not the whole host. Today §10 launches a device CC
> entry with copied arguments/entropy; §5.4 budgets bind device invocations,
> §3.3/§10 handles belong to a loaded program/run, and §7.3/§10 tapes cover
> device R effects. These require explicit container-to-object/root/certificate
> association, host-entry-to-device-entry bindings, budget/handle lifetime
> negotiation and tape/run association in a versioned mixed-launch contract.
> ISA §7.1 defines device memory but §10 defines no general host-memory-by-reference
> launch ABI: that contract must add address/layout mapping, range/liveness,
> aliasing, ownership/synchronisation, writeback and reversal rules, preserving
> dialect §§6.6, 8.5, 19.4–19.5. Until published, these are explicit
> bennett-4ht boundary obligations, not grounds to require whole-binary
> lowering or omit mixed execution.

## Present assumptions and exact publication changes

| Contract object | Assumption today | Exact change needed for mixed use |
|---|---|---|
| Object and linking (§§9.1–9.3) | One ISA object's closed sections and linked symbols; no native-container schema. | Publish a versioned inventory associating embedded/linked ISA object bytes and object_hash with selected device roots and certificates. A native-container hash, if used, is a separate identity. |
| Certificates/inventory (§§12.1–12.3) | Closure-keyed device-region records; current text does not explicitly delimit native inventory. | Insert the shared scoping rule in §12.3, cross-reference from §9.2, and require complete device-owner/link-proof coverage per root. Multiple device roots can share static closures without merging root-relative contexts. |
| Launch/entry (§10) | bennett_run(program, cc_entry, args, entropy) starts device CC; it is not native main. | Publish a host call/return shim and container/root selection/feature negotiation. Define native argument/result marshalling, including ptr (whose host binding §10 does not specify), status/fault propagation, suspend/reverse/close and ownership. Do not invent a current general launch shim. |
| Budgets (§5.4; §§10–11) | Validated token tuples and identity scoped to a program/run; action_call has no primary token (§4.7). | Bind selected device invocation budgets and token equality to the launch request; charge actual device/replay work. State host/shim accounting separately; a native process has no invented global B. |
| Handles (§3.3; §10; dialect §6.6) | Register ids refer to the loaded program's ledger; run ownership and token validity are explicit. | Map host-visible opaque handles to that program/ledger with validity and cross-launch ownership checks; preserve class vectors and token identities. Do not serialize native pointers as device ids or token tuples as identities. |
| Effect tape (§7.3; §10) | Run-associated R effects and explicit copied hostcall requests; no implicit log of native host I/O. | Associate each device run and tape with the launched root and define host effect/proxy ordering and replay at the boundary, preserving §7.4 reversal. Saved-journal interchange needs its own versioned replay profile. |
| Host memory by reference (§§7.1–7.2; dialect §§8.5, 17.1, 19.4–19.5) | Device layout plus dialect snapshot/replay and boundary-address/promotion rules; copied hostcall bytes do not provide general shared pointers. | Publish the mapping and lifetime protocol named in the statement, including allocator metadata/history and pointer-rich layouts, so ordinary host memory can be supplied safely without universal host promotion. Retain existing snapshot/replay as a construction, not the sole eventual capability. |

For example native main can call marked roots A and B in distinct ISA objects.
Each has its own object/root certificate and root context []; neither
certificate inventories main or host-only libc. A shared linked device
callee is checked in each root's actual invocation tree. A native comparison
executes the corresponding materialised region/callee closure under its
recorded layout, input and tool configuration (dialect §§17.7, 19.5, 19.9).

These notes specify boundary scope and exact remaining contract work; they
implement no container, host shim or memory registration. The existing
bennett-4ht issue tracks that publication/implementation obligation. Current
device contracts and their complete estimate/proof requirements remain in
force; an unimplemented boundary is not evidence that mixed execution works.


# The document under review (round 2 snapshot)

# 4ta — one interface slot placed across several classes

Council proposal for `b-isa-spec-4ta` (P1).

## Round-1 blocking items and resolution

Read the [round-1 report](../../../../bennett/docs/council/pass2-4ta-2026-11-02/round-1/report.md)
and both `out-sol.json` and `out-codex.json` in that directory. The table
maps every blocking item to this revision; it claims a proposed resolution,
not a new council verdict.

| Item | Change and witness |
|---|---|
| sol 4ta-B1; codex B1 — §3.3 says one class per register but concat has no same-class condition | Replace the register/ledger definition and specify new/concat/slice/free/reversal and frame-binding vector equality below. Witness: new A[1], new B[1], concat yields [A,B], split maps 0 to 3; inverse execution restores original wires, classes, ids and handle states. |
| sol 4ta-B2; codex B2 — missing mixed-object descriptor scope | Append the §9.4 rule below. A native host launches a device CC wrapper that joins the two registers and calls the single split slot; host functions remain outside B5. |

## Defect and North Star scope

ISA 0.9 §11 “Topology placement” supplies one vertex **per bit**, in
§9.4 slot order, and callees inherit actual operand placements. §8.3
assigns a class to each vertex. Neither clause requires all bits of a
source interface slot to have the same class. B5 (§9.4), however, gives
each slot exactly one `class` and its full signature `width`; unknown
fields and duplicate slot identities are not an extension mechanism
(§9.1). A legal mixed placement has no truthful descriptor. This is a
contract defect under C4/C5/C8, distinct from [v2v](b-isa-spec-v2v.md):
4ta concerns different bits **within one invocation of one slot**.

North Star §§4.1/4.3/4.4 promises virtual memory, allocation classes and
target-driven placement/resource reporting. Splitting a virtual slot
must not require a user rewrite or a target with a larger homogeneous
class. Class capacities still constrain physical feasibility; no capacity
is manufactured by this repair.

## Alternatives and worked witness

An action `split(t:wire[2] mut)` applies `x t[0]` and `x t[1]`.
A target has two vertices, 0:A and 1:B, each class capacity 1, and exact
single-wire X entries of duration 1 for either class. Placement
`interface_vertices:[[0,1]]` is legal: there is no multi-wire adjacency
requirement to satisfy. Both bits stay live for two sequential slots.
For unit class rates, time=2, peak[A]=peak[B]=1 and
qubit_second[A]=qubit_second[B]=2. On the four values t=0,1,2,3, the
outputs are respectively 3,2,1,0; the adjoint restores every input.

One B5 record `class:A,width:2` lies about bit 1 and exceeds A's capacity;
class B has the symmetric problem. Two `mut:0` records lose uniqueness;
two new source slots change the interface and its hashes unnecessarily.

| Alternative | Tradeoff |
|---|---|
| Ordered per-bit class vector | Precise, but repeats one class name per bit. |
| Ordered maximal class fragments | Precise, compact for runs, retains the source slot. Recommended. |
| Require a homogeneous class or split the source signature | Removes this legal placement or changes the program interface. |

## Recommended exact spec diff

First replace §3.3's singular-class register definition and update every
ledger-frame reference to class as follows (including §10's allocation
cross-reference):

```text
A register denotes an ordered vector of n device-resident wires with a
resolved target class for each wire. The register ledger records that
class vector, width, nonzero id and live/viewed/consumed state. A single
class is shorthand for a homogeneous vector. qreg.new cls n and
bennett_qreg_new allocate n wires of cls and remain homogeneous requests.
qreg.concat accepts differing operand classes and concatenates their
wire and class vectors in operand order, with no movement, copy,
measurement or reallocation. Its existing distinct/live/non-view
preconditions and consumption/new-id transitions remain unchanged.
qreg.slice selects the corresponding wire/class subsequences, with its
existing view ownership and one-call consumption rule. A slice never
relabels a wire. Zero-width runtime registers have empty vectors.
Ledger frames retain enough information to restore the complete class
vectors, wire ordering, identities and handle states. In particular free
records/restores the vector along with the same id/width and cleanliness
status; concat reversal restores both operands and their prior vectors;
slice reversal restores the parent/view states. New-frame reversal keeps
its existing cleanliness/exclusivity hypothesis.
Binding a CC/R register to a region slot requires its ordered class
vector to equal that slot's selected, expanded §9.4 class vector, and,
with topology, its actual vertices to equal the inherited placement.
Check this with the existing width/disjointness preflight before any
effect or view consumption. Class vectors introduce no new alias or
storage-sharing permission, nor permission to relabel or move a wire.
```

Then in §9.4 replace the `slots` record production with:

```text
{id, role, width, class} |
{id, role, width, fragments:[{offset, width, class}, ...]}
```

Append:

```text
Each source slot still has exactly one record, its original id and role,
and its complete signature width. Exactly one of class and fragments
is present. class abbreviates one fragment at offset 0 covering width.
For the fragmented form, fragment offsets and widths are nonnegative
and positive integers respectively, using §9.1 integer encodings.
Fragments are in increasing offset order, start at 0, meet without gaps
or overlap, and end at the instantiated slot width. Each class is a
resolved declared target class. Adjacent equal-class fragments must be
merged; use the class form when there is only one fragment.

Flatten bundle bits LSB first, or region cells in address order and
each cell LSB first, exactly as for the existing slot. A fragment names
the class of that half-open bit interval; it is not a new source slot,
allocation, lifetime, alias or permission to share storage. Repeated
slot ids, wrong roles/widths, gaps, overlaps or out-of-range fragments
reject. Slot widths retain §8.1's c-only expression rules; fragment
widths/offsets describe the resolved fixed signature width.

With topology, every bit's fragment class must equal the class of its
§11 vertex; vertices and simultaneous live uses remain disjoint. A
callee's fragments must equal the inherited operand-class vector. In
the absence of topology, they bind that resolved vector without naming
vertices. Capacity, occupancy and peak accounting use each bit's actual
class, and charge no wire twice. Existing homogeneous records and
scratch/history/borrow/ancilla schemas retain their meanings.
```

The witness descriptor is one slot
`{id:"mut:0",role:"mut",width:2,fragments:[{offset:0,width:1,class:"A"},`
`{offset:1,width:1,class:"B"}]}`. This is proposed record notation;
the production's field names are exact. It is not accepted B5 today.

## Fat-object applicability

Incorporate [the shared scoping statement](fat-object-scope.md#citable-scoping-statement).
Append the following proposed normative paragraph to §9.4:

```text
In a mixed fat object, B5 descriptors cover device-region invocations
in the embedded or linked ISA objects, bound to their object, closure,
schedule and invocation identities. Native host functions and process
state require no ISA frame slots or certificate records. A native host
may launch a device CC entry under §10; that entry may concatenate
homogeneous A and B registers under §3.3 and bind their ordered class
vector to one source slot. The fragments describe that binding without
splitting its signature. Native main, argv/envp and host-only libc stay
outside the device-frame inventory. This rule supplies no new container
or general host-memory launch ABI.
```

The shared statement lists today's assumptions and the exact remaining
container, launch, budget, handle, tape and host-memory contract changes.
This note does not assume that the device object is the entire binary.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

The register-class rule is a core clarification/extension requiring renewed
core council review; B5 alone cannot make the slot usable. The
slot/signature/source closure stays the same for the unchanged split body. A repaired certificate
changes bytes; its existing placement schedule and target need not.
No reference recipe, leaf ordinal or catalog hash changes. Circuits,
scheduler, estimator and checker must validate fragments against the same
bit ordering and retain class-qualified resource rows. This does not
alter the explicit class of a source `qalloc` or add routing.

## Register construction and reversal witness

In a device CC wrapper allocate a=qreg.new A 1 and b=qreg.new B 1,
then t=qreg.concat a b and action_call @split (t) () () (). The two
homogeneous allocations each fit capacity 1. Concat consumes a,b and
creates a fresh t over the same ordered wires with classes [A,B]; its
mut:0 descriptor expands to exactly that vector. From initial clean 0,
the call produces 3; on 0,1,2,3 it gives 3,2,1,0. Reverse the action,
then concat, to restore a,b live with their original ids, [A]/[B]
classes and wire order, with t no longer live; reversing allocations
then releases their clean wires under the existing ledger rule.
A slice of t inherits its selected class subsequence. A false [A,A]
frame fails before effects rather than relabelling the B wire.

## Conformance vectors and verification

Test the four outputs and inverse above, both topology and class-only
descriptors, a region[1,2] slot with four flattened bits, and a homogeneous
legacy record. Reject a gap, overlap, width zero, split duplicate id,
incorrect class for a vertex, reversed offset order and adjacent unmerged
runs. For region[1,2] use vertices/classes [A,B,A,B], capacity 2 per class;
offsets 0,1,2,3 denote cell0.bit0, cell0.bit1, cell1.bit0, cell1.bit1.
For a controlled invocation enlarge the target to vertices 0:A,1:B,2:C,
capacity 1 each, edges [0,2] and [1,2], and exact controlled-X leaves of
duration 1. The control is caller-owned, outside mut:0;
(c,t) maps to (c,t XOR (3*c)), time=2 and occupancy=2/peak=1 in each class. Exact finite arithmetic is
checked by [check_rev2.py](check_rev2.py); no target/scheduler implementation
is executed here.

The originating note cites circuits' `docs/design/2026-11-01-m5-contract-requests.md`,
“Heterogeneous interface-slot frames”. Its reported refusal records
incompleteness, not a new legality rule for source programs.

## Release and route

Recommend a **0.9 K/B5 encoding extension** alongside v2v; the gaps are
independent and both require vectors. Waiting for 0.10 leaves an explicit
blocker for complete 0.9 certificates, not permission to omit mixed
placements. Keep 4ta open for council and consumer work.

## Publication and validation boundary

This is a revision proposal, not council acceptance or a consumer implementation.
The default publication rule in spec/README.md requires a full version bump
for semantic changes. The recommended 0.9 K route requires explicit council
and maintainer authorization of its in-version compatibility rules; this note
does not grant it. All unchanged-hash claims assume unchanged header/source
and catalog envelopes. A full version publication recomputes affected symbol,
object, target, schedule, certificate and versioned ref-library identities and
consumer pins even when recipe bodies stay fixed. Published 0.9 bytes remain
available; older closed-schema readers require an upgrade for new fields.

The reproducible finite and document checks are in [check_rev2.py](check_rev2.py):
`timeout 120 python3 -I -B /home/tobias/Projects/bennett-workspace/bennett-isa-spec/docs/notes/2026-11-02-rev2/check_rev2.py`.
Executed for this revision: exit 0, all eight PASS result lines.
These finite checks verify witnesses and revision structure; they implement
no proposed-format checker or host shim. No full repository gate or sibling gate is claimed: scripts/pre-push
uses git/worktree writes, forbidden by this runner, and its setup changes paths
outside this job. Underlying beads remain open for publication and consumers.

# Beads

Use Beads as the shared project task system. Local plans, scratch files, and personal memories are useful, but they are not the durable source of truth for project work.

## First Step

Run:

```bash
bd prime
```

If that prints nothing, check whether the repository has an active Beads workspace:

```bash
bd where
```

## Preferred Route

Use the `bd` CLI when shell access is available. It is the most compact and direct Beads interface.

## Core CLI Workflow

1. Find work:

```bash
bd ready
bd list --status=open
bd list --status=in_progress
```

2. Inspect before editing:

```bash
bd show <id>
```

3. Claim work atomically:

```bash
bd update <id> --claim
```

4. Create durable follow-up work when implementation reveals new tasks:

```bash
bd create "Short title" --description="Why this exists and what needs to be done" --type=task --priority=2
```

5. Close completed work:

```bash
bd close <id> --reason="Completed"
```

## What Belongs In Beads

Use Beads for:

- shared project tasks
- blockers and dependencies
- discovered follow-up work
- work that must survive thread reset, compaction, or handoff
- status that another person or agent should be able to resume

Use agent-local planning tools only for the current turn's execution checklist. Do not treat them as shared project state.

## Rules

- Do not create markdown TODO files as the source of truth when Beads is available.
- Do not use `bd edit`; it opens an interactive editor. Use `bd update` flags instead.
- Prefer `--json` when parsing `bd` output programmatically.
- If hooks are installed, `bd prime` may already be injected. Run it manually when context is missing.
- Do not auto-close or mutate tasks unless the work is actually complete.

469:| `qreg.free q` | q live, not a view, clean on every basis configuration (a verifying executor checks it; on failure q stays live and unchanged; a non-verifying one: §17 item 14) | q consumed |
470:| `q2 = qreg.slice q lo len` | q live, not a view; `lo` and `len` each a literal (`len` ≥ 1) or an `i64` value, both unsigned; lo + len ≤ n, computed without wrap (a trap even when literals make the failure evident) | q2 a view of wires [lo, lo+len) of q, of type `qreg[len]` for a literal len and `qreg[*]` otherwise; q viewed; q2 MUST be the operand of exactly one region call and of nothing else; after that call q2 is consumed and q is live again; a use of q while it is viewed traps (the static rule below covers the cases suite F rejects). 471:| `q = qreg.concat q1 q2` | q1, q2 live, not views, distinct registers | q1, q2 consumed; q live, with a fresh id, over the wires of q1 then q2; `qreg[n1+n2]` if both operands are static, else `qreg[*]` |

856:| `uncall` | `uncall @f (args) (results)` | **segment uncall**, legal exactly under §7.4: the most recent completed call segment of the history is a call of `@f` whose recorded boundary argument and result values equal the current operand values, every frame after it is pure, and it contains, at any nesting depth, no `measure` frame and no `qreg.free` frame whose cleanliness was not verified; otherwise CC trap / R fault `FAULT_UB_TRAP` with no state change beyond the fault record. The segment is reversed by the cancellation-aware history traversal of §7.4 (LIFO, nested segments included; frames already reversed and `uncall` markers skipped) and its frames are marked reversed; the `uncall` itself pushes a frame naming the segment |
858:| `fault` | `fault C` | CC and R; literal 0 ≤ C < 2^64; one terminal record with code C for C ≠ 0, else 4 (§5.4); run ends `FAILED`, no successor executes, prefix history retained for reversal (§7.4, §10, §14.1). 995:### 4.5 Copy, XOR, exchange, in-place
1003:| `qinplace` | `qinplace @g @ginv tk x (ins..) (mem..) (args..)`; written `qinplace @g @ginv tk x` when all three lists are empty (and only then) | **Well-formed** iff `@g` and `@ginv` are oracles whose `in` slots are x's width n followed by the widths of `ins` in order, whose `tgt` width is n, whose fault widths are equal (F), whose `mem` slots match the `mem` regions (role `in`, §3.3's matching rule) and whose `args` types are those of `args`; x, the `ins` wires and the effective control context are pairwise disjoint. **Sem**: `t = qalloc any n; f = qalloc any F; oracle_call @g tk (x, ins..) t f (mem..) (args..); qexch x t; oracle_call @ginv tk (x, ins..) t f (mem..) (args..); qfree t; qfree f`, so x ← g(x; ins, mem, args); these are its generated operations, in this order (§11). **Inverse-pair obligation**: under the tuple of `tk`, the call's `args` values and the tuples of the tokens among them, on every basis value of x, of the `ins` wires and of the `mem` cells, both oracles are fault-free and g(·; ins, mem, args) and ginv(·; ins, mem, args) are mutually inverse permutations of the n-bit encoding. It is carried by an `inverse_pairs` entry (§12.3) of the certificate record of the region whose body contains the `qinplace`; an entry `{g: G, ginv: H}` covers both `qinplace @G @H` and `qinplace @H @G` (its claim checks both compositions). An entry **covers** a `qinplace` when its `budget` is componentwise ≤ the tuple of `tk` (success at a tuple implies the same result at every larger one, §5.4), every token-typed argument's tuple equals the entry's `arg_tokens` tuple for that argument (equality: a nested oracle's outcome may change with its budget), and every other `args` value lies in its claim's `args` domain. Its claim is an `exhaustive` claim with `expected: "inverse_pair"`, which the executor verifies as a checker does (once per closure hash, before first use), or a `structural` `inverse_pair_library` claim, which covers when the named claim of the linked library's certificate is such a claim for the same closure hashes of g and ginv (no re-execution; a library claim without an `args` domain covers every value; its proof or evidence label is the library claim's); a `sampled` claim never covers. Before any effect of a CC/R region call, every `qinplace` the callee executes (transitively; its token and `args` are resolved from the call's classical arguments, which reach lifted calls only as classical arguments, §4.2) MUST have a covering entry, else CC trap / R fault `FAULT_UB_TRAP`. The private t and f are not values of the region for §6: the certified pair cleans them, the only cleanup that is not a §6.4 realisation. Under control the allocations are unconditional and every gate of both calls and of the exchange is controlled |

## Core Rules
- **Default**: Use beads for ALL task tracking (`bd create`, `bd ready`, `bd close`)
- **Prohibited**: Do NOT use TodoWrite, TaskCreate, or markdown files for task tracking
- **Workflow**: Create beads issue BEFORE writing code, mark in_progress when starting
- **Memory**: Use `bd remember "insight"` for persistent knowledge across sessions. Do NOT use MEMORY.md files — they fragment across accounts. Search with `bd memories <keyword>`.
- Persistence you don't need beats lost context
- Default: do not commit, push, or run dolt remote sync without explicit authority. Team-maintainer behavior is opt-in and still subordinate to user/orchestrator instructions.
- Git workflow: conservative by default; commit/push only with explicit user/orchestrator or team-maintainer authority
- Session management: check `bd ready` for available work

## Essential Commands

### Finding Work
- `bd ready` - Show issues ready to work (no blockers)
- `bd list --status=open` - All open issues
- `bd list --status=in_progress` - Your active work
- `bd show <id>` - Detailed issue view with dependencies

### Creating & Updating
- `bd create --title="Summary of this issue" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=task|bug|feature --priority=2` - New issue
  - Priority: 0-4 or P0-P4 (0=critical, 2=medium, 4=backlog). NOT "high"/"medium"/"low"
- `bd create ... --parent=<id>` - Hierarchical child (task under epic, subtask under task; inherits parent labels)
- `bd update <id> --claim` - Claim work
- `bd unclaim <id>` - Release stuck issue (agent crashed)
- `bd update <id> --assignee=username` - Assign to someone
- `bd update <id> --if-assignee=<expected> --assignee=<new>` - Atomic reassign: applies only if the assignee still matches (--if-status=<expected> guards status; --if-assignee='' requires unassigned). Mismatch exits non-zero with nothing written — never retry blindly
- `bd update <id> --title/--description/--notes/--design` - Update fields inline
- `bd close <id>` - Mark complete
- `bd close <id1> <id2> ...` - Close multiple issues at once (more efficient)
- `bd close <id> --reason="explanation"` - Close with reason
- **Tip**: When creating multiple issues/tasks/epics, use parallel subagents for efficiency
- **WARNING**: Do NOT use `bd edit` - it opens $EDITOR (vim/nano) which blocks agents

### Dependencies & Blocking
- `bd dep add <issue> <depends-on>` - Add dependency (issue depends on depends-on)
- `bd blocked` - Show all blocked issues
- `bd show <id>` - See what's blocking/blocked by this issue

### Sync & Collaboration
- `bd search <query>` - Search issues by keyword

### Project Health
- `bd stats` - Project statistics (open/closed/blocked counts)
- `bd doctor` - Check for issues (sync problems, missing hooks)
- `bd doctor --check=conventions` - Check for convention drift (lint, stale, orphans)

### Quality Tools
- `bd create --validate` - Check description has required sections
- `bd create --acceptance="criteria"` - Set acceptance criteria (checked by --validate)
- `bd create --design="decisions"` - Record design decisions
- `bd create --notes="context"` - Add supplementary notes
- `bd config set validation.on-create warn` - Auto-validate on every create
- `bd lint` - Check existing issues for missing sections

### Lifecycle & Hygiene
- `bd defer <id> --until="date"` - Defer work to a future date
- `bd supersede <id> --with=<new-id>` - Mark issue as superseded
- `bd close <id> --suggest-next` - Show newly unblocked issues after closing
- `bd stale` - Find issues with no recent activity
- `bd orphans` - Find issues with broken dependencies
- `bd preflight` - Pre-PR checks (lint, stale, orphans)
- `bd human <id>` - Flag for human decision (list/respond/dismiss)

### Structured Workflows
- `bd formula list` - See available workflow templates
- `bd mol pour <name>` - Start structured workflow from formula

# 4ta — one interface slot placed across several classes

Council proposal for `b-isa-spec-4ta` (P1).

## Round-1 blocking items and resolution

Read the [round-1 report](../../../../bennett/docs/council/pass2-4ta-2026-11-02/round-1/report.md)
and both `out-sol.json` and `out-codex.json` in that directory. The table
maps every blocking item to this revision; it claims a proposed resolution,
not a new council verdict.

| Item | Change and witness |
|---|---|
| sol 4ta-B1; codex B1 — §3.3 says one class per register but concat has no same-class condition | Replace the register/ledger definition and specify new/concat/slice/free/reversal and frame-binding vector equality below. Witness: new A[1], new B[1], concat yields [A,B], split maps 0 to 3; inverse execution restores original wires, classes, ids and handle states. |
| sol 4ta-B2; codex B2 — missing mixed-object descriptor scope | Append the §9.4 rule below. A native host launches a device CC wrapper that joins the two registers and calls the single split slot; host functions remain outside B5. |

## Defect and North Star scope

ISA 0.9 §11 “Topology placement” supplies one vertex **per bit**, in
§9.4 slot order, and callees inherit actual operand placements. §8.3
assigns a class to each vertex. Neither clause requires all bits of a
source interface slot to have the same class. B5 (§9.4), however, gives
each slot exactly one `class` and its full signature `width`; unknown
fields and duplicate slot identities are not an extension mechanism
(§9.1). A legal mixed placement has no truthful descriptor. This is a
contract defect under C4/C5/C8, distinct from [v2v](b-isa-spec-v2v.md):
4ta concerns different bits **within one invocation of one slot**.

North Star §§4.1/4.3/4.4 promises virtual memory, allocation classes and
target-driven placement/resource reporting. Splitting a virtual slot
must not require a user rewrite or a target with a larger homogeneous
class. Class capacities still constrain physical feasibility; no capacity
is manufactured by this repair.

## Alternatives and worked witness

An action `split(t:wire[2] mut)` applies `x t[0]` and `x t[1]`.
A target has two vertices, 0:A and 1:B, each class capacity 1, and exact
single-wire X entries of duration 1 for either class. Placement
`interface_vertices:[[0,1]]` is legal: there is no multi-wire adjacency
requirement to satisfy. Both bits stay live for two sequential slots.
For unit class rates, time=2, peak[A]=peak[B]=1 and
qubit_second[A]=qubit_second[B]=2. On the four values t=0,1,2,3, the
outputs are respectively 3,2,1,0; the adjoint restores every input.

One B5 record `class:A,width:2` lies about bit 1 and exceeds A's capacity;
class B has the symmetric problem. Two `mut:0` records lose uniqueness;
two new source slots change the interface and its hashes unnecessarily.

| Alternative | Tradeoff |
|---|---|
| Ordered per-bit class vector | Precise, but repeats one class name per bit. |
| Ordered maximal class fragments | Precise, compact for runs, retains the source slot. Recommended. |
| Require a homogeneous class or split the source signature | Removes this legal placement or changes the program interface. |

## Recommended exact spec diff

First replace §3.3's singular-class register definition and update every
ledger-frame reference to class as follows (including §10's allocation
cross-reference):

```text
A register denotes an ordered vector of n device-resident wires with a
resolved target class for each wire. The register ledger records that
class vector, width, nonzero id and live/viewed/consumed state. A single
class is shorthand for a homogeneous vector. qreg.new cls n and
bennett_qreg_new allocate n wires of cls and remain homogeneous requests.
qreg.concat accepts differing operand classes and concatenates their
wire and class vectors in operand order, with no movement, copy,
measurement or reallocation. Its existing distinct/live/non-view
preconditions and consumption/new-id transitions remain unchanged.
qreg.slice selects the corresponding wire/class subsequences, with its
existing view ownership and one-call consumption rule. A slice never
relabels a wire. Zero-width runtime registers have empty vectors.
Ledger frames retain enough information to restore the complete class
vectors, wire ordering, identities and handle states. In particular free
records/restores the vector along with the same id/width and cleanliness
status; concat reversal restores both operands and their prior vectors;
slice reversal restores the parent/view states. New-frame reversal keeps
its existing cleanliness/exclusivity hypothesis.
Binding a CC/R register to a region slot requires its ordered class
vector to equal that slot's selected, expanded §9.4 class vector, and,
with topology, its actual vertices to equal the inherited placement.
Check this with the existing width/disjointness preflight before any
effect or view consumption. Class vectors introduce no new alias or
storage-sharing permission, nor permission to relabel or move a wire.
```

Then in §9.4 replace the `slots` record production with:

```text
{id, role, width, class} |
{id, role, width, fragments:[{offset, width, class}, ...]}
```

Append:

```text
Each source slot still has exactly one record, its original id and role,
and its complete signature width. Exactly one of class and fragments
is present. class abbreviates one fragment at offset 0 covering width.
For the fragmented form, fragment offsets and widths are nonnegative
and positive integers respectively, using §9.1 integer encodings.
Fragments are in increasing offset order, start at 0, meet without gaps
or overlap, and end at the instantiated slot width. Each class is a
resolved declared target class. Adjacent equal-class fragments must be
merged; use the class form when there is only one fragment.

Flatten bundle bits LSB first, or region cells in address order and
each cell LSB first, exactly as for the existing slot. A fragment names
the class of that half-open bit interval; it is not a new source slot,
allocation, lifetime, alias or permission to share storage. Repeated
slot ids, wrong roles/widths, gaps, overlaps or out-of-range fragments
reject. Slot widths retain §8.1's c-only expression rules; fragment
widths/offsets describe the resolved fixed signature width.

With topology, every bit's fragment class must equal the class of its
§11 vertex; vertices and simultaneous live uses remain disjoint. A
callee's fragments must equal the inherited operand-class vector. In
the absence of topology, they bind that resolved vector without naming
vertices. Capacity, occupancy and peak accounting use each bit's actual
class, and charge no wire twice. Existing homogeneous records and
scratch/history/borrow/ancilla schemas retain their meanings.
```

The witness descriptor is one slot
`{id:"mut:0",role:"mut",width:2,fragments:[{offset:0,width:1,class:"A"},`
`{offset:1,width:1,class:"B"}]}`. This is proposed record notation;
the production's field names are exact. It is not accepted B5 today.

## Fat-object applicability

Incorporate [the shared scoping statement](fat-object-scope.md#citable-scoping-statement).
Append the following proposed normative paragraph to §9.4:

```text
In a mixed fat object, B5 descriptors cover device-region invocations
in the embedded or linked ISA objects, bound to their object, closure,
schedule and invocation identities. Native host functions and process
state require no ISA frame slots or certificate records. A native host
may launch a device CC entry under §10; that entry may concatenate
homogeneous A and B registers under §3.3 and bind their ordered class
vector to one source slot. The fragments describe that binding without
splitting its signature. Native main, argv/envp and host-only libc stay
outside the device-frame inventory. This rule supplies no new container
or general host-memory launch ABI.
```

The shared statement lists today's assumptions and the exact remaining
container, launch, budget, handle, tape and host-memory contract changes.
This note does not assume that the device object is the entire binary.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

The register-class rule is a core clarification/extension requiring renewed
core council review; B5 alone cannot make the slot usable. The
slot/signature/source closure stays the same for the unchanged split body. A repaired certificate
changes bytes; its existing placement schedule and target need not.
No reference recipe, leaf ordinal or catalog hash changes. Circuits,
scheduler, estimator and checker must validate fragments against the same
bit ordering and retain class-qualified resource rows. This does not
alter the explicit class of a source `qalloc` or add routing.

## Register construction and reversal witness

In a device CC wrapper allocate a=qreg.new A 1 and b=qreg.new B 1,
then t=qreg.concat a b and action_call @split (t) () () (). The two
homogeneous allocations each fit capacity 1. Concat consumes a,b and
creates a fresh t over the same ordered wires with classes [A,B]; its
mut:0 descriptor expands to exactly that vector. From initial clean 0,
the call produces 3; on 0,1,2,3 it gives 3,2,1,0. Reverse the action,
then concat, to restore a,b live with their original ids, [A]/[B]
classes and wire order, with t no longer live; reversing allocations
then releases their clean wires under the existing ledger rule.
A slice of t inherits its selected class subsequence. A false [A,A]
frame fails before effects rather than relabelling the B wire.

## Conformance vectors and verification

Test the four outputs and inverse above, both topology and class-only
descriptors, a region[1,2] slot with four flattened bits, and a homogeneous
legacy record. Reject a gap, overlap, width zero, split duplicate id,
incorrect class for a vertex, reversed offset order and adjacent unmerged
runs. For region[1,2] use vertices/classes [A,B,A,B], capacity 2 per class;
offsets 0,1,2,3 denote cell0.bit0, cell0.bit1, cell1.bit0, cell1.bit1.
For a controlled invocation enlarge the target to vertices 0:A,1:B,2:C,
capacity 1 each, edges [0,2] and [1,2], and exact controlled-X leaves of
duration 1. The control is caller-owned, outside mut:0;
(c,t) maps to (c,t XOR (3*c)), time=2 and occupancy=2/peak=1 in each class. Exact finite arithmetic is
checked by [check_rev2.py](check_rev2.py); no target/scheduler implementation
is executed here.

The originating note cites circuits' `docs/design/2026-11-01-m5-contract-requests.md`,
“Heterogeneous interface-slot frames”. Its reported refusal records
incompleteness, not a new legality rule for source programs.

## Release and route

Recommend a **0.9 K/B5 encoding extension** alongside v2v; the gaps are
independent and both require vectors. Waiting for 0.10 leaves an explicit
blocker for complete 0.9 certificates, not permission to omit mixed
placements. Keep 4ta open for council and consumer work.

This is a revision proposal, not council acceptance or a consumer implementation.
The default publication rule in spec/README.md requires a full version bump
for semantic changes. The recommended 0.9 K route requires explicit council
and maintainer authorization of its in-version compatibility rules; this note
does not grant it. All unchanged-hash claims assume unchanged header/source
and catalog envelopes. A full version publication recomputes affected symbol,
object, target, schedule, certificate and versioned ref-library identities and
consumer pins even when recipe bodies stay fixed. Published 0.9 bytes remain
available; older closed-schema readers require an upgrade for new fields.

# Mixed fat object — shared contract scope

Proposal for every revision in this directory; not a published ISA amendment.
Clause references are to [ISA 0.9](../../../spec/Bennett-ISA-Spec-0.9.md),
[the North Star](../../../../bennett/docs/Bennett-NorthStar.md), and, where
qualified, the maintained dialect 0.2/0.3 texts. This is the shared statement
required by bennett-4ht and the council rubric's standing mixed-fat-object check.

## Citable scoping statement

> A mixed fat object retains an ordinary native host binary and embeds ISA
> objects for marked device modules and their linked device closures (North
> Star §§2, 3.3, 4.4). ISA §§9.1–9.3 identify each device object and selected
> root; §12.3 certificates, owner records and reports cover that closure,
> with a separate binding/context set per device root and [] relative to
> that root (§12.1). Native entry, argv/envp and host-only libc need no ISA
> region, frame or certificate record; native comparison binds the selected
> materialised closure, not the whole host. Today §10 launches a device CC
> entry with copied arguments/entropy; §5.4 budgets bind device invocations,
> §3.3/§10 handles belong to a loaded program/run, and §7.3/§10 tapes cover
> device R effects. These require explicit container-to-object/root/certificate
> association, host-entry-to-device-entry bindings, budget/handle lifetime
> negotiation and tape/run association in a versioned mixed-launch contract.
> ISA §7.1 defines device memory but §10 defines no general host-memory-by-reference
> launch ABI: that contract must add address/layout mapping, range/liveness,
> aliasing, ownership/synchronisation, writeback and reversal rules, preserving
> dialect §§6.6, 8.5, 19.4–19.5. Until published, these are explicit
> bennett-4ht boundary obligations, not grounds to require whole-binary
> lowering or omit mixed execution.

## Present assumptions and exact publication changes

| Contract object | Assumption today | Exact change needed for mixed use |
|---|---|---|
| Object and linking (§§9.1–9.3) | One ISA object's closed sections and linked symbols; no native-container schema. | Publish a versioned inventory associating embedded/linked ISA object bytes and object_hash with selected device roots and certificates. A native-container hash, if used, is a separate identity. |
| Certificates/inventory (§§12.1–12.3) | Closure-keyed device-region records; current text does not explicitly delimit native inventory. | Insert the shared scoping rule in §12.3, cross-reference from §9.2, and require complete device-owner/link-proof coverage per root. Multiple device roots can share static closures without merging root-relative contexts. |
| Launch/entry (§10) | bennett_run(program, cc_entry, args, entropy) starts device CC; it is not native main. | Publish a host call/return shim and container/root selection/feature negotiation. Define native argument/result marshalling, including ptr (whose host binding §10 does not specify), status/fault propagation, suspend/reverse/close and ownership. Do not invent a current general launch shim. |
| Budgets (§5.4; §§10–11) | Validated token tuples and identity scoped to a program/run; action_call has no primary token (§4.7). | Bind selected device invocation budgets and token equality to the launch request; charge actual device/replay work. State host/shim accounting separately; a native process has no invented global B. |
| Handles (§3.3; §10; dialect §6.6) | Register ids refer to the loaded program's ledger; run ownership and token validity are explicit. | Map host-visible opaque handles to that program/ledger with validity and cross-launch ownership checks; preserve class vectors and token identities. Do not serialize native pointers as device ids or token tuples as identities. |
| Effect tape (§7.3; §10) | Run-associated R effects and explicit copied hostcall requests; no implicit log of native host I/O. | Associate each device run and tape with the launched root and define host effect/proxy ordering and replay at the boundary, preserving §7.4 reversal. Saved-journal interchange needs its own versioned replay profile. |
| Host memory by reference (§§7.1–7.2; dialect §§8.5, 17.1, 19.4–19.5) | Device layout plus dialect snapshot/replay and boundary-address/promotion rules; copied hostcall bytes do not provide general shared pointers. | Publish the mapping and lifetime protocol named in the statement, including allocator metadata/history and pointer-rich layouts, so ordinary host memory can be supplied safely without universal host promotion. Retain existing snapshot/replay as a construction, not the sole eventual capability. |

For example native main can call marked roots A and B in distinct ISA objects.
Each has its own object/root certificate and root context []; neither
certificate inventories main or host-only libc. A shared linked device
callee is checked in each root's actual invocation tree. A native comparison
executes the corresponding materialised region/callee closure under its
recorded layout, input and tool configuration (dialect §§17.7, 19.5, 19.9).

These notes specify boundary scope and exact remaining contract work; they
implement no container, host shim or memory registration. The existing
bennett-4ht issue tracks that publication/implementation obligation. Current
device contracts and their complete estimate/proof requirements remain in
force; an unimplemented boundary is not evidence that mixed execution works.

## Publication and combined schema decisions

“0.9 K repair” means the existing council-reviewed repair track, not
permission to alter a published semantic contract silently. `spec/README.md`
normally requires a full-version bump for semantic changes. Council must
choose that publication route and explicit subformat/ABI compatibility
rules. Every note's unchanged-source-hash statement assumes an authorized
in-version repair with unchanged source envelope bytes. A version bump
changes all affected version-prefixed envelopes and consumer pins. The
ref catalog changes only if its own versioned bytes change; none of these
proposals needs a silent rewrite of `ref@0.9`.

Use one context vocabulary across v2v/cwq/jw7: `context_key` is exactly
§12.1's concrete invocation chain in the bound root realisation, including
instance, call_index, loop iterations and applicable oracle step/phase.
The root key is []; nested default-covered calls are included. Resolve
variant/controls/arguments/budget/placements from that tree. Order arrays
by the unsigned UTF-8 bytes of each key's JCS encoding. Frame/resource/
fault arrays validate the same context identities where applicable;
variant/control-count alone is never an invocation identifier. K12 exact
rationals and outward resource display remain unchanged. ysw separately
adds the missing token equality relation; it does not change params_hash
or serialize process ids.

Recommended acceptance groups are applicability/owner binding (0jq),
frames and reports (4ta/v2v/cwq/jw7), borrow proofs (7s0), token context
(ysw), and native models (95w). The fixture and migration dispositions
should proceed with their existing owners. K numbers are assigned only
at acceptance, avoiding collisions with concurrent council work.

```python
from pathlib import Path
from itertools import product
from fractions import Fraction as F
import copy

assert Path.cwd() == ROOT

assert os.environ['CARGO_TARGET_DIR'] == str(SCRATCH / 'target')
assert not any(k in os.environ for k in
               ('DISPLAY', 'WAYLAND_DISPLAY', 'DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS', 'BROWSER'))

isa = ['0jq', '4ta', 'v2v', '7s0', '95w', '0n5', 'jw7', 'cwq',
       'ysw', '552', 'ojy']
ids = ['b-isa-spec-' + x for x in isa] + [
       'b-dialect-spec-2cg', 'b-dialect-spec-djm']
assert {p.name for p in OUT.iterdir()} == {x + '.md' for x in ids} | {'INDEX.md'}
for ident in ids:
    p = OUT / (ident + '.md')
    s = p.read_text()
    for title in ('Defect', 'North Star', 'Alternatives',
                  'Recommended exact spec diff', 'Hashes', 'Conformance', 'Release'):
        assert title.lower() in s.lower(), (ident, title)
    assert ident in s and not re.search(r'\b(?:TODO|TBD)\b', s)
for p in OUT.glob('*.md'):
    s = p.read_text()
    assert sum(line.startswith('```') for line in s.splitlines()) % 2 == 0, p
    # Remove fenced examples before treating Markdown syntax as links.
    prose = re.sub(r'```[^\n]*\n.*?\n```', '', s, flags=re.S)
    for link in re.findall(r'\[[^\]]+\]\(([^)]+)\)', prose):
        if not link.startswith(('http://', 'https://', '#')):
            assert (p.parent / link.split('#')[0]).exists(), (p, link)
index = (OUT / 'INDEX.md').read_text()
for ident in ids:
    assert index.count('| [' + ident + ']') == 1, ident
print('PASS: 13 per-bead notes and index; required sections, fences and links')

# 0jq: H = M/sqrt(2), so M*M=2I exactly; each H column has two nonzeros.
M = ((1, 1), (1, -1))
assert [[sum(M[i][k]*M[k][j] for k in range(2)) for j in range(2)]
        for i in range(2)] == [[2, 0], [0, 2]]
assert all(sum(M[i][j] != 0 for i in range(2)) == 2 for j in range(2))
# 95w: Bell +/- have norm squared 2 before division by sqrt(2), overlap zero.
plus, minus = [1, 0, 0, 1], [1, 0, 0, -1]
assert sum(a*b for a, b in zip(plus, minus)) == 0
assert sum(a*a for a in plus) == sum(a*a for a in minus) == 2
assert F(1)-F(1) == 0

# 4ta, v2v, cwq: exact basis maps and separate root/child footprints.
for t in range(4):
    assert (t ^ 3) ^ 3 == t
for t, u in product(range(2), repeat=2):
    assert ((t ^ 1) ^ 1, (u ^ 1) ^ 1) == (t, u)
assert (2, 1*2, 1*2, 1, 1) == (2, 2, 2, 1, 1)  # split time, A/B occupancy/peak
child_footprints = [(1, 1, 1), (1, 2, 2)]  # time, occupancy, peak
root = (2, 2*2, 2)
assert root == (2, 4, 2)
assert sum(c[2] for c in child_footprints) != root[2]
assert root[1] + sum(c[1] for c in child_footprints) != root[1]
assert F(1) + F(1,4) == F(5,4) and F(2) + F(1,4) == F(9,4)

# 7s0: a finite model of K76's all-operand stack rule for these CX-only prefixes.
def entry_c(sequence):
    values = {'a': None, 'x': None, 'c': 0}
    stacks = {w: [] for w in values}
    events = []
    for control, target in sequence:  # cx and mcx(1) already normalized to one kind
        identity = (control, target)
        operands = (control, target)
        top = stacks[control][-1] if stacks[control] else None
        if (top is not None and all(stacks[w] and stacks[w][-1] == top
                                   for w in operands)
                and events[top][0] == identity):
            before = events[top][1]
            for w in operands:
                stacks[w].pop()
                values[w] = before[w]
        elif values[control] == 0:
            continue  # disabled: no causal event
        else:
            event = len(events)
            events.append((identity, {w: values[w] for w in operands}))
            for w in operands:
                stacks[w].append(event)
            values[target] = (values[target] ^ values[control]
                              if all(values[w] is not None for w in operands)
                              else None)
    return values['c']
assert entry_c([]) == 0
assert entry_c([('a','c'), ('a','c')]) == 0
assert entry_c([('a','c'), ('x','c'), ('a','c'), ('x','c')]) is None
def frame_ok(c_values):
    return all((c, b ^ c) == (c, b) for c in c_values for b in range(2))
assert frame_ok([0]) and not frame_ok([0, 1])
assert not frame_ok(sorted(set([0] + [1])))  # repeated-visit D_F union cannot be narrowed
entry_products = [(0,0), (1,1)]
assert all(b ^ c ^ d == b for c,d in entry_products for b in range(2))
assert not all(b ^ c ^ d == b for c,d,b in product(range(2), repeat=3))
assert sum(a*b for a, b in zip((1,1), (1,-1))) == 0  # phase on B changes |+>

# 0n5: legal state size versus sample count, without allocating it.
assert (1 << 63) // 8 == (1 << 60)
# jw7: all input/target/fault tuples; F_B's code, not final XOR word.
for budget, expected_faults in [(1, 256), (2, 0)]:
    faults = 0
    for x, target, fault in product(range(4), range(4), range(16)):
        payload, code = (0, 1) if budget == 1 else ((x+1) % 4, 0)
        after = (x, target ^ payload, fault ^ code)
        assert 0 <= after[1] < 4 and 0 <= after[2] < 16
        faults += code != 0
    assert faults == expected_faults
assert 1 ^ 1 == 0 and 1 ^ 0 == 1

# ysw: identity partitions are independent of canonical tuple values.
def partition(handles):
    seen, result = {}, []
    for h in handles:
        if h not in seen:
            seen[h] = len(seen)
        result.append(seen[h])
    return result
def valid_labels(labels):
    return (all(type(x) is int and x >= 0 for x in labels)
            and labels == partition(labels))
assert partition([10,20,20]) == [0,1,1]
assert partition([10,20,30]) == [0,1,2]
assert partition([20,20]) == [0,0] and partition([]) == []
assert all(not valid_labels(x) for x in ([1,1], [0,2], [0,-1]))
args = [['1','0','0','0'], ['1','0','0','0']]
def jcs(x):
    return json.dumps(x, sort_keys=True, separators=(',', ':'), ensure_ascii=False).encode()
params = hashlib.sha256(jcs(args)).hexdigest()
assert params == hashlib.sha256(jcs(copy.deepcopy(args))).hexdigest()
assert jcs({'args':args, 'token_aliases':[0,0]}) != jcs(
           {'args':args, 'token_aliases':[0,1]})
assert (4 if partition([20,20]) == [0,0] else 3) == 4
assert (4 if partition([20,30]) == [0,0] else 3) == 3

# 2cg: exact primitive request/bump history; production metadata stores are not simulated.
def allocation_trace(production, limit):
    bump, records = 4096, []
    def alloc(n):
        nonlocal bump
        if bump + max(n, 1) > 4096 + limit:
            raise OverflowError
        p = bump
        records.append((p, n))
        bump += max(n, 1)
        return p
    payload, code = None, 0
    try:
        alloc(3)
        if production:
            alloc(0)
        padding = (-bump) % 16
        if padding:
            alloc(padding)
        payload = alloc(16)
        if production:
            alloc(32)
    except OverflowError:
        code = 2
    return payload, bump, records, code
minimal = allocation_trace(False, 32)
production = allocation_trace(True, 64)
assert minimal == (4112, 4128, [(4096,3),(4099,13),(4112,16)], 0)
assert production == (4112, 4160,
                      [(4096,3),(4099,0),(4100,12),(4112,16),(4128,32)], 0)
failed = allocation_trace(True, 32)
assert failed == (4112, 4128, production[2][:-1], 2)
assert 16+32 == 32+16 == 48 and min(16,48) != min(32,48)
# djm: required public name/environment examples, not a musl process simulation.
assert '/bin/demo'.rsplit('/',1)[-1] == 'demo'
assert '/bin/'.rsplit('/',1)[-1] == ''
assert dict(s.split('=',1) for s in ['K=V'])['K'] == 'V'
print('PASS: structural, frame/resource, borrow, census, token, allocation and process witnesses')

                       capture_output=True, timeout=120)
    assert p.returncode == 0, (argv, p.returncode, p.stdout, p.stderr)
    return p.stdout
fixture = ROOT / 'conformance/fixtures/controlled-action-09/controlled.bisa'

print('PASS: published B5/K13 check/hash identity and 4 forward/4 adjoint action cases')

# ojy: independent written-order sweep of the current 54 bare-formal recipes.
catalog_path = ROOT / 'spec/ref-0.9.json'
raw = catalog_path.read_bytes()
recipes = {r['kind']:r for r in json.loads(raw)['recipes']}
pin_ref = '5de4e4995350313e50217880ba1b95de39cd043283b726337d4894efb005d197'
assert hashlib.sha256(b'isa=0.9\nexpansion=ref\nversion=0.9\n'+raw+b'\n').hexdigest() == pin_ref
body_interfaces = {}
for kind, recipe in recipes.items():
    if not all(isinstance(p,str) for p in recipe['parameters']):
        continue
    used = []
    def walk(body, bound):
        for node in body:
            if 'gate' in node:
                for wire in node['wires']:
                    name = wire.split('[',1)[0]
                    if name not in bound and name not in used:
                        used.append(name)
            elif 'alloc' in node:
                bound.add(node['alloc'])
            elif 'for' in node:
                walk(node['body'], bound | {node['for']})
            elif 'inverse' in node:
                walk(node['body'], bound)
            elif 'release' in node:
                assert node['release'] in bound
            else:
                raise AssertionError((kind, node))  # no unexamined constructors
    walk(recipe['body'], set(recipe['parameters']))
    body_interfaces[kind] = used
assert len(body_interfaces) == 54
changed = [k for k,v in body_interfaces.items() if v != sorted(v)]
assert len(changed) == 31
assert body_interfaces['value.select'] == ['p','b','a','t']

      'edge interface arrays differ; all other fields identical; both probes 86/86 and 16/16')

# spec/

`Bennett-ISA-Spec.md` is the normative text: draft 0.8, **accepted by the
three-model council on 2026-10-28 (round 8, all members accept, no blocking
issues, mean 4.17/5)**. Council records and the runner live in the `bennett`
meta repo (`docs/council/isa-2026-10-28/`, `tools/council.py`).

Rules for changing it:

- Every semantic change bumps the full version string (section 1); drafts
  are not envelope-compatible, so the four example hashes in section 16.3
  change with it. Run `python3 reference-interpreter/isa_check.py --repatch`
  then `python3 reference-interpreter/isa_check.py`.
- Surgical edits only; a rewrite has introduced a mechanical error every
  time it was tried.
- Add every new example or stated result to `isa_check.py` before anyone
  grades it.
- Section 17 lists decisions deferred to implementation with their
  defaults; an implementer who must choose something not covered there
  files an issue and the default goes into section 17 first.
- Revisions go back through the council.

## 3. Machine model

### 3.1 Tiers and the control invariant

```
  CC ──calls──▶ R functions (call/uncall)       CC ──calls──▶ Q regions
  R  ──calls──▶ R functions, Q regions          Q  ──calls──▶ Q regions (§5.5); oracle bodies call lprocs
```

CC runs on the device's classical-control processor; the host reaches
the device only through §10. **No instruction in any tier makes a
control or scheduling decision that depends on the value of a wire**
except through predication (§3.5). Gate angles are finite `f64` literals
or classical arguments fixed at region entry.

**Call-tier policy** (suite F). The diagram, completed with same-tier
calls; a call, uncall or region call outside this table is ill-formed:

| Caller | `call` (§4.1) | `uncall` of a completed segment (§7.4) | region calls (§4.7) | lifted calls (§4.2) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CC function | CC and R functions | of a CC or R callee | oracles, actions | – |
| R function | R functions | of an R callee | oracles, actions | – |
| oracle body, lproc | – | – | – | `lift.call` of lprocs; `lift.oracle_call`/`lift.oracle_uncall` of oracles; `lift.perm_call` of permutation actions |
| action | – | – | oracles, actions (§5.3, §5.5) | – |

An R function never calls a CC function: CC code may measure and make
host requests (§4.1), which an R history cannot reverse.

### 3.2 State

| Component | Tier | Contents |
|---|---|---|
| classical environment | CC, R | dynamic SSA instances (§7.4), block parameters, activation stack, **quantum-register handles** (`qreg[n]`, `qreg[*]`, §3.3), tokens (§5.4) |
| register and token ledgers | CC, R | every register's id, class, width and state (§3.3); every device-minted token's tuple and validity (§5.4) |
| classical memory | CC, R | byte-addressed, little-endian, sparse; allocation table (§7.1) |
| effect tape, entropy tape | R | §7.3 |
| run history | CC, R | one frame per executed instruction (§7.4) |
| wire store | Q (CC and R via `qreg`) | live wires with roles `input target fault borrowed mutable workspace location cell` |
| quantum memory | Q | regions of `wire[W]` cells: local (§4.6) or interface (§5.1) |
| register file | Q | locations of the active oracle and its lproc closure (§3.7) |
| predicate stack | Q | wires; the active predicate is their conjunction |
| oracle step state | Q | `pc, pc' : wire[P]`; `faulted : wire`; `code : wire[F]`; `result_val : wire[T]`; counters (§3.7); B frames |
| budget | Q | classical: B, heap_limit, stack_limit, depth_limit |

### 3.3 Types, ownership, operand schema

```
  iN (1 ≤ N ≤ 128)   ptr (width ptr_width)   f64   token   qreg[n]   qreg[*]   region[A,W]
  wire   wire[N]      linear values in structured code and interfaces
  reg[N]              a location (oracle bodies, lprocs); not linear
```

**Widths.** The bound 1 ≤ N ≤ 128 applies to the type `iN` only (a lifted
row's `.iN` suffix names a location width, §4.2). `wire[N]`,
`reg[N]` and `qreg[n]` have N, n ≥ 1 and `region[A,W]` has A ≥ 0, W ≥ 1,
with no upper bound (a `qreg[*]` register's width is a run-time value n
≥ 0, §3.3 below); a value of these types is a bit-vector of its
width in every semantics (§14.1), including interface bundles,
pseudo-locations, locations, cells, stack cells and frame slots.

**Linear SSA with implicit versioning** (structured code and CC region
calls): a `wire`/`wire[N]` value is produced once and consumed once;
mode `in` consumes and re-produces a successor of the same name;
`consume` consumes; `produce` creates; a name is not used after
`consume`. **Regions are threaded the same way.** **Indexed operand**
`w[i]` (literal i) borrows wire i for one instruction.

**Disjointness.** The wire operands of one instruction are pairwise
distinct; **every wire of the effective control context is distinct from
every target and from every consumed or produced operand** of an
instruction executed under it (so `qpred p; x p` is ill-formed); a
predicate wire is not modified between its push and its matching pop; a
`qif`/`qloop … while` selector is not modified inside its arms or body.
A region's interface bundles and regions are pairwise disjoint.
Distinctness is judged on the wires denoted: a bundle and an indexed
operand of it, or two indexed operands denoting the same wire, overlap
and are not distinct. [deps: ISA-3.3-P3, ISA-4.4]

**Per-opcode operand schema** (§4.8): every opcode has a tier set, a
fixed arity, operand kinds and a result type; results are written first
(`w = op a b`), modes are never written, and **result types that are not
determined by the operands are written as a suffix** (`r = const.i32 5`,
`r = zext.i64 a`, `r = trunc.i8 a`, `r = ptrtoint.i64 p`, `p = inttoptr
a`). Width rules: `binop`: `iN` operands of equal width; `icmp`: `iN` or
`ptr` operands of equal width and the same type (CC/R; lifted forms:
§4.2); `select` arms equal width; `zext`/`sext` strictly widen, `trunc`
strictly narrows; `load.iN`
and `lift.load.iN` require N ∈ {8, 16, …, 128}; `measure` of `qreg[n]`
with n ≤ 128 yields `in`, and `measure.iN` (1 ≤ N ≤ 128) of a `qreg[*]`
yields `iN`; signed division truncates toward zero. The
literal c of `const.iN c` and `lift.const.iN c` satisfies −2^(N−1) ≤ c <
2^N and denotes c mod 2^N (both the signed and the unsigned spelling of
every value are accepted); any other literal is ill-formed (suite F).

**Lifted width rules** (suite F; a mismatch is ill-formed, never a
truncation or an extension). Every address operand (`a` of `lift.load`/
`lift.store`, `p` of `lift.boundscheck`/`lift.free`) and every address
result (`w` of `lift.addr`/`lift.alloc`) is `reg[ptr_width]`; the length
`n` of `lift.alloc`/`lift.boundscheck` is a location of any width or a
literal in [0, 2^ptr_width); `inrange` and every prospective sum are
computed at width max(widths of the operands involved) + 1 with no wrap.
The destination of `lift.label` and the operand of `qjumpr` have any
width N with 2^N > n (the index-space size). `lift.call` location
arguments have exactly the callee's parameter widths and its destination the callee's
result width; its token, classical and region lists match the callee's
corresponding groups in count, order and type (§3.7); `lift.ret v` has
the containing lproc's result width;
`lift.oracle_call`/`lift.oracle_uncall`/`lift.perm_call` operands have
exactly the callee signature widths, and `mem` operands name caller
regions whose `[A,W]` equal the callee's and whose role is `in` for an
`in` slot (an `in` region is never passed to a `mut` slot); `fault C` and
`lift.faultif p C` require the literal C < 2^F, where F is the fault
width of the containing oracle or, in an lproc, of every oracle whose
lproc closure contains it (checked at load and again at link; an lproc
in no oracle's closure is checked when linked into one). The equal-width and widening/narrowing constraints above apply to lifted rows on their location and pseudo-location bit-vectors as specified in §4.2; the CC/R iN and ptr operand-type restrictions do not retype lifted locations. The destinations of one `lift.parmov` are pairwise distinct.

**Quantum registers in CC and R.** A **register** is n device-resident
wires of one class with a nonzero 64-bit **id**, recorded in the
program's **register ledger** with its class, width and state:
**live**, **viewed** (one slice of it is live) or **consumed**.
`qreg[n]` is the type of a register whose width n is fixed statically;
`qreg[*]` the type of a register whose width is known only at run time
(an unsigned 64-bit n ≥ 0). A `qreg[n]` value may be used wherever a
`qreg[*]` is expected; a `qreg[*]` value bound to a `qreg[n]` parameter
or block parameter (`call`, `br`, `ret`, launch) or to a slot of width n
(region call, §4.7) is checked at that transfer: its width MUST equal
the required one. The `qreg.*` instructions execute in CC and R;
`measure` only in CC. An instruction whose precondition fails is a CC
trap / R fault (§4.9) with code `FAULT_UB_TRAP` unless the table says
otherwise, and has no effect. An id's value is chosen by the executor
(the suites compare ids only for equality); an id is never reassigned to
another register while the program is loaded. **Handle transitions:**

| Instruction | Precondition | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| `q = qreg.new cls n` | `n` a literal n ≥ 1 (q: `qreg[n]`) or an `i64` value read unsigned, 0 included (q: `qreg[*]`); the device can provide n wires of class `cls`, else code `FAULT_BUDGET_HEAP` | q live, clean, with a fresh id and wires disjoint from every live or viewed register. [deps: ISA-3.3-P7] |
| `qreg.free q` | q live, not a view, clean on every basis configuration (a verifying executor checks it; on failure q stays live and unchanged; a non-verifying one: §17 item 14) | q consumed |
| `q2 = qreg.slice q lo len` | q live, not a view; `lo` and `len` each a literal (`len` ≥ 1) or an `i64` value, both unsigned; lo + len ≤ n, computed without wrap (a trap even when literals make the failure evident) | q2 a view of wires [lo, lo+len) of q, of type `qreg[len]` for a literal len and `qreg[*]` otherwise; q viewed; q2 MUST be the operand of exactly one region call and of nothing else; after that call q2 is consumed and q is live again; a use of q while it is viewed traps (the static rule below covers the cases suite F rejects). Reversing that region call (§7.4) restores q viewed and q2 live; reversing the slice frame then restores q live |
| `q = qreg.concat q1 q2` | q1, q2 live, not views, distinct registers | q1, q2 consumed; q live, with a fresh id, over the wires of q1 then q2; `qreg[n1+n2]` if both operands are static, else `qreg[*]` |
| `w = qreg.width q` | q live, not a view | w: `i64`, the width; no state change |
| `id = qreg.id q` | q live, not a view | id: `i64`, the bits of q's id; no state change |
| `q = qreg.from_id id` | `id` is the id of a register of the program that is live, not a view, and not owned by another run that is not CLOSED (§10) | q: `qreg[*]`, an alias of that register; no ledger state change; the run now owns the register (§10), which reversal does not undo (ownership ends when the run is CLOSED) |
| region call (§4.7) with q as operand | q live (a view included); its width equals the slot's (§3.3 binding); the wire sets filling distinct wire/region slots are pairwise disjoint, and an explicit control is disjoint from targets and consumed/produced operands (read-only input overlap remains allowed), else CC trap / R fault FAULT_UB_TRAP before any effect, including any view consumption. [deps: ISA-3.3-P4, ISA-4.7, ISA-4.9] | q unchanged. [deps: ISA-4.7] |
| `r = measure q` / `r = measure.iN q` | q live, not a view; the form `measure.iN` (1 ≤ N ≤ 128) for a `qreg[*]`, whose width MUST be N (checked before measuring); for a `qreg[n]` either `measure` or `measure.iN` with N = n (checked statically) | q consumed, its wires released; r: `in` / `iN` |
| reversal of a `qreg.*` frame (§7.4, §10) | – | the ledger state before that frame (below). A `measure` frame is **never reversed**: a segment containing one cannot be uncalled (§7.4) and a run containing one is not reversible (§10) |

**Ledger frames and their reversal** (§7.4). A `qreg.new` frame records
the id, class and width; its reversal releases that register without a
test: every later frame touching it has been undone first (LIFO) and the
run owns it exclusively (§10), so under T4's exactness hypothesis it is
clean, and otherwise its residual, within the declared bounds of the
undone realisations (§12.3), is discarded with it.
A `qreg.free` frame records the id, class, width and whether its
cleanliness was verified; its reversal re-creates the register clean and
live with the same id, class and width (every alias sees it live
again). A `qreg.slice` frame records the parent id, lo and len, a
`qreg.concat` frame the two operand ids and the new id; their reversal
restores the exact prior states and wire order without moving, copying
or measuring a wire. `qreg.width`, `qreg.id` and `qreg.from_id` change
no ledger state and their frames are pure (§7.4); if an `uncall` later
removes the register an alias refers to, every use of the alias traps.

**Binding**: a register of width n (`qreg[n]`, or `qreg[*]` of run-time
width n) fills a `wire[n]` slot bit for bit (bit 0 ↔ wire 0); it fills a
`region[A,W]` slot iff n = 2^A·W with cell i = bits [iW, (i+1)W); for a
`qreg[*]` the width is checked at the call, before any effect.
Host-supplied register ids are `qreg` values of the parameter's type.

The register ids supplied as arguments of one `bennett_run` MUST
denote pairwise disjoint registers, else `bennett_run` returns `E_ARG`
before executing the entry function or acquiring any register ownership.
[deps: ISA-3.3-P7, ISA-10]

**Registers, handles and aliases.** A `qreg` value is a reference to a
register of the device; the state live / viewed / consumed belongs to
the register, not to a name. `qreg` values pass unchanged as `call`
arguments, `ret` values and `br` block arguments (each transfer binds
another name to the same register and changes no state; a `qreg[*]`
reaching a `qreg[n]` parameter is width-checked, above). Two names bound
to one register (by such transfers or by `qreg.from_id`) are
**aliases**: a transition
made through either is observed through both. The wire operands of one
region call, with its effective control context, MUST denote pairwise
disjoint wires (§3.3 disjointness); aliases violate it. **Static and
run-time verdicts.** Suite F rejects exactly: two `qreg` operands of one
region call or of one `qreg.concat` that are the same name; and, on some path from a
`qreg.slice`, a use of the view's name other than as the operand of one
region call (a `br`, `call` or `ret` transfer included), its reaching a
`ret` or the end of the function unconsumed, or a use of the slice's
parent operand name before that call. Every other overlap, and every
use of a viewed register through another name, is a CC trap / R fault
with code `FAULT_UB_TRAP` before any effect.

### 3.4 Execution in CC and R
